It controls epistemic status and containment, never evidence visibility or presumptive acceptance/rejection.","legacy_docket_assessment":{"adjudicable":false,"packet":null,"status":"invalid"},"packet_scope_relation":"exact_declared_pericope","reader_identity":[{"focus_ref":"109:2","identity_status":"legacy_unbound","packet_identity":null,"protocol":"focus-trace-hermetic-response-v4","reader_id":"reader_hft_a","trace_kind":"reconstructed"}]},"reader_synthesis_count":10,"source_present":true,"structured_insight_count":14,"unstructured_record_count":0},"identity":{"ayah_ref":"109:2","lane":"global","linguistic_source_ref":"109:2","surface_ref":"109:2","unit_kind":"numbered_ayah"},"primary_floor":{"source_ref":"109:2","target_tokens":[["Ben",["109:2:2"]],["sizin",["109:2:4"]],["taptıklarınıza",["109:2:3","109:2:4"]],["tapmam",["109:2:1","109:2:2"]]],"text":"Ben sizin taptıklarınıza tapmam."},"schema_version":"commentary-v5-hermetic-scope-packet-v1","scope":{"hft":{"assigned_record_count":10,"authoring_policy":{"every_assigned_record_requires_explicit_review":true,"parseable_hft_is_always_visible_to_authoring":true,"provenance_affects_qualification_not_visibility":true,"scope_mismatch_affects_lane_assignment_not_visibility":true,"unresolved_branch_citations_do_not_suppress_records":true},"authoring_status":"visible_with_provenance_qualification"},"lane_contract":"reader walks and cross-run publications with explicit trust labels","pericope":{"ayah_from":1,"ayah_to":6,"id":"s109-p01-001-006","label":"Whole surah","number":1,"refs":["109:1","109:2","109:3","109:4","109:5","109:6"]},"readiness":{"authoring_effect":"Docket readiness is reported for source provenance. HFT visibility is governed by the authoring policy above.","docket_mode":"quarantine_without_hft","docket_ready":true}},"selected_context_units":[],"support_registry":[{"branch_refs":["root_000973/B003"],"citable":false,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"global","source_local_id":"reader_a:activated_readings_md:2","source_type":"v12_reader_walks_wide","support_id":"sup_036338e4b74bf607e97e","text":"2. **This is the ongoing-action layer of a larger temporal matrix.** Fixed **أَعْبُدُ / تَعْبُدُونَ** use verbal imperfect forms of `ع ب د B003`; 109:3 and 109:5 shift the addressees to the participle **عَٰبِدُونَ**, and 109:4 opposes the speaker’s participle to their perfect **عَبَدتُّمْ**. The fixed verbs carry ongoing or recurrent enactment, while the later forms supply role and completed-practice contrasts. Aspectual force is an inference from the supplied morphology, not a packet gloss. The line now reads as one deliberately selected temporal layer, rather than an exhaustive repetition of the surrounding denials.","trust":"legacy_unbound"},{"branch_refs":["root_000973/B005"],"citable":false,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"global","source_local_id":"reader_a:activated_readings_md:3","source_type":"v12_reader_walks","support_id":"sup_08c39ffdabae5c85807b","text":"3. **The ayah rejects a paved/trodden path.** ع ب د B005, \"physical subduing and smoothing,\" is activated by the repeated identical worship-root across 109:2-5 and the final د ي ن B005, \"habit and customary state.\" Mechanism: the addressees' worship is a route already made smooth by repeated habit; the speaker's negation refuses to tread that passable path. The abductive move is mine: the packet gives \"smoothed road\" and \"custom\" branches; I infer path-dependence from repetition and habit. Reading change: the ayah becomes spatial and practical, a refusal to walk an already worn ritual road.","trust":"legacy_unbound"},{"branch_refs":["root_000973/B001","root_000973/B004"],"citable":true,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"global","source_local_id":"finding-2","source_type":"cross_run_publication","support_id":"sup_0c4c25c96711f9e6c6f6","text":"{\"anchors\":[[\"109:2:2\",\"root_000973\",[\"B001\",\"B004\"]],[\"109:2:4\",\"root_000973\",[\"B001\",\"B004\"]]],\"grade\":\"strong\",\"text\":\"İbadet etmeyi reddetmek, başka bir düzenin denetimi ve sahipliği altına girmeyi de reddetme boyutu kazanır.\"}","trust":"trusted"},{"branch_refs":["root_000973/B004","root_000973/B005"],"citable":false,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"global","source_local_id":"reader_a:retrospective_surprises_md:1","source_type":"v12_reader_walks","support_id":"sup_19500bd6a17d1bf0db1f","text":"The later noun forms in 109:3 and 109:5 corrected the first reading of 109:2. This ayah is primarily about present action, because both worship occurrences are verbs; the following participles show that role/identity will be handled separately. 109:4 made ع ب د B005 stronger in retrospect, since عَبَدتُّمْ introduced prior practice and allowed the \"smoothed path\" image to become historical habit rather than mere metaphor. 109:6 activated the legal/social side of ع ب د B004 by pairing worship with د ي ن B004, so the initial refusal also reads as refusal of capture into another possession-order.","trust":"legacy_unbound"},{"branch_refs":["root_000973/B003"],"citable":false,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"global","source_local_id":"reader_a:activated_readings_md:1","source_type":"v12_reader_walks","support_id":"sup_26a7990b1cb532dae41f","text":"1. **Present refusal of submissive worship.** The fixed ayah supplies ع ب د B003, \"worship and submissive obedience,\" twice, once in the first-person verb أَعْبُدُ and once in the second-person verb تَعْبُدُونَ. The activators are the prior address \"ٱلْكَٰفِرُونَ\" in 109:1 and the later دِين in 109:6. Mechanism: the first negation marks the speaker's worship-act as non-overlapping with the addressees' worship-act. The abductive move is mine: the packet gives two matched verbs and later religious allotment; I infer that the shared root highlights incompatible objects rather than shared practice. Reading change: the line reads not as \"I do not worship in general\" but as a first boundary around a specific obedience relation.","trust":"legacy_unbound"},{"branch_refs":["root_000504/B004"],"citable":false,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"global","source_local_id":"reader_a:retrospective_surprises_md:1","source_type":"v12_reader_walks_wide","support_id":"sup_2d9c4260f67205533a89","text":"109:3–5 made the two imperfect verbs retrospectively non-exhaustive: participles and the perfect exposed 109:2 as the ongoing-action row in a form-by-person matrix. Then 109:6 supplied two unexpected consequences. Its `د ي ن B004` sharpened the fixed `ع ب د B001/B004` branches into a control relation, and its `د ي ن B005` gave the material road of `ع ب د B005` a social-temporal mechanism as repeated service becoming custom.","trust":"legacy_unbound"},{"branch_refs":["root_000973/B003"],"citable":true,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"global","source_local_id":"finding-1","source_type":"cross_run_publication","support_id":"sup_49ca0e3edae6ba4ec4c0","text":"{\"anchors\":[[\"109:2:2\",\"root_000973\",[\"B003\"]],[\"109:2:4\",\"root_000973\",[\"B003\"]]],\"grade\":\"strong\",\"text\":\"Çevresindeki isim cümlelerinden farklı olarak bu ayet, ayrılığı yerleşik kimlikten önce süren ve tekrarlanan eylem düzeyinde kurar.\"}","trust":"trusted"},{"branch_refs":["root_000973/B003"],"citable":false,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"global","source_local_id":"reader_a:activated_readings_md:1","source_type":"v12_reader_walks_wide","support_id":"sup_4c46f1f7e960dcaa2176","text":"1. **The action class is shared while its object is not.** Both fixed verbs activate `ع ب د B003`, worship and submissive obedience, and both are imperfect verbs; person and the two sides of **ما** change, but the root does not. Repetition assigns a common service category to speaker and addressees, while **لَا** blocks identity across their object relations. The inferred move is that, because the action-root is held constant, the operative difference lies in what governs that action. The ayah thus denies interchangeability of allegiance, not the existence of service on either side.","trust":"legacy_unbound"},{"branch_refs":["root_000973/B008"],"citable":true,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"global","source_local_id":"finding-4","source_type":"cross_run_publication","support_id":"sup_81a1b9e6dcacc8cea3a7","text":"{\"anchors\":[[\"109:2:2\",\"root_000973\",[\"B008\"]],[\"109:2:4\",\"root_000973\",[\"B008\"]]],\"grade\":\"reject\",\"text\":\"Keskin olumsuzlukta öfkeli bir kaçınma duyulabileceği önerisi, biçimsel tekrarın ölçülü tonu nedeniyle oldukça belirsizdir.\"}","trust":"trusted"},{"branch_refs":["root_000973/B004"],"citable":false,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"global","source_local_id":"reader_a:activated_readings_md:2","source_type":"v12_reader_walks","support_id":"sup_8b774354f3f7e24602ac","text":"2. **The speaker refuses to enter a subjugating system.** ع ب د B004, \"making someone a slave,\" is activated by the second-person plural act تَعْبُدُونَ and by د ي ن B004 in 109:6, \"subjugation and possession.\" Mechanism: what the addressees worship is read as a regime that can enslave or reduce the participant, not only as an object of ritual attention. The abductive move is mine: the packet gives slavery/subjugation branches in ع ب د and د ي ن; I infer that worship can create a social-legal relation of capture. Reading change: لَا أَعْبُدُ becomes refusal of capture into another order of ownership.","trust":"legacy_unbound"},{"branch_refs":["root_000973/B008"],"citable":false,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"global","source_local_id":"reader_a:activated_readings_md:4","source_type":"v12_reader_walks","support_id":"sup_902870caf012feadaa3a","text":"4. **Affective aversion is possible but secondary.** ع ب د B008, \"prideful aversion and anger,\" is activated by the blunt initial لَا and by the direct address of 109:1. Mechanism: the negation carries an emotional edge of offended refusal, but the surrounding formulaic repetition restrains it into declaration rather than outburst. The abductive move is mine: the packet gives the aversion branch and repeated negation; I infer affect from sharp syntax while limiting it by structure. Reading change: the refusal can sound morally/affectively resistant, not merely propositional.","trust":"legacy_unbound"},{"branch_refs":["root_000973/B005"],"citable":true,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"global","source_local_id":"finding-3","source_type":"cross_run_publication","support_id":"sup_92ffdbe52f340995ada1","text":"{\"anchors\":[[\"109:2:2\",\"root_000973\",[\"B005\"]],[\"109:2:4\",\"root_000973\",[\"B005\"]]],\"grade\":\"weak\",\"text\":\"Tekrarlanan ibadet, alışkanlıkla yürünmesi kolaylaşmış bir yol gibi belirir; konuşan bu yerleşik yola girmeyi reddeder.\"}","trust":"trusted"},{"branch_refs":["root_000504/B004","root_000973/B001"],"citable":false,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"global","source_local_id":"reader_a:activated_readings_md:3","source_type":"v12_reader_walks_wide","support_id":"sup_ee4885895482e35aa367","text":"3. **Refusal of worship can be refusal of another regime’s control.** Fixed **عبد** also has `ع ب د B001` (owned status) and `ع ب د B004` (making someone a slave); 109:6 activates the corresponding `د ي ن B004` image of subjugation and possession. The object split in the fixed ayah supplies two allegiance targets, and the final possessive allocation supplies two bounded orders. I infer the causal arrow that sustained submissive service can install a relation of control. Under that social mechanism, **لَا أَعْبُدُ** refuses entry into the addressees’ governing ownership, not only performance of a different rite.","trust":"legacy_unbound"},{"branch_refs":["root_000973/B005"],"citable":false,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"global","source_local_id":"reader_a:activated_readings_md:4","source_type":"v12_reader_walks_wide","support_id":"sup_fcb9cbf9ee4ed9a354dc","text":"4. **Repeated service wears a customary path.** Fixed `ع ب د B005` supplies the material image of a subdued, smoothed, passable road, while final `د ي ن B005` supplies habit and customary state. The imperfect repetition provides recurrent practice; the two branches provide path and custom. The packet does not state that repetition smooths the route, so that causal arrow is abductive. The fixed denial can consequently be heard as refusal to enter the path made easy and normal by the addressees’ repeated service.","trust":"legacy_unbound"},{"anchor_evidence":[],"anchor_evidence_coverage":{"boundary":"Exact Arabic verifies surface contact only. HFT-stated segmentation, word indices, roots, branches, and roles remain attributed nominations unless independently supplied elsewhere in this packet.","cited_anchor_count":0,"exact_arabic_is_surface_evidence_only":true,"missing_anchor_refs":[],"supplied_anchor_count":0,"target_morphology_supplied":false},"branch_refs":[],"payload":"The shared worship verb became a disputed relation of obedience and allegiance rather than only two cultic acts.","qualification":{"anchor_scope_complete":true,"authoring_effect":"Source qualifications affect epistemic status and containment, never visibility or presumptive outcome.","packet_scope_relation":"exact_declared_pericope","reader_identity_status":"legacy_unbound","record_warnings":["Unanchored reader synthesis must be assessed as one inventory item, not accepted as a surah thesis."]},"role":"hft_nomination_evidence","scope":"global","source_local_id":"reader_hft_a:reader_summary:strongest_changes/0","source_type":"hft","support_id":"sup_67e4fb7d2f0099f494a4","trust":"legacy_unbound"},{"anchor_evidence":[],"anchor_evidence_coverage":{"boundary":"Exact Arabic verifies surface contact only.
